### Q44. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of an ideal monatomic gas undergoes a cyclic process as shown in the figure. If the temperature of the gas at state <math><mn>1</mn></math> is <math><mn>300</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> and at state <math><mn>4</mn></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>, then heat exchanged during process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>→</mo><mn>3</mn></math>, is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/image__3_.png" style="width: 200px; height: 129px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>750</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>800</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mn>23</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>nC</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#8211;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>The processes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>4</mn></math> are isochoric in nature. Hence, for these processes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>. As a result, we obtain,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mn>23</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>400</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q45. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>An inverted bell, lying at the bottom of lake <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>47</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> deep, has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> of air trapped in it. The bell is brought to the surface of the lake, then the volume of the trapped air will become [atmospheric pressure = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>70</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hg</mi></math> and density of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hg</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>]</p><p> </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to Boyle's law, pressure and volume are inversely proportional to each other i.e. <math> <mrow> <mtext>p</mtext> <mo>∝</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>v</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0010163_AnsExp2_1.jpg" /></strong></u>

<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mo>(</mo> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>h</mtext> <msub> <mi mathvariant="normal">ρ</mi> <mtext>w</mtext> </msub> <mtext>g</mtext> <mo>)</mo> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub><mo>=</mo> <mo>(</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>h</mtext> <msub> <mi mathvariant="normal">ρ</mi> <mtext>w</mtext> </msub> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>)</mo> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>


<math> <mtable columnalign="left"> <mtr> <mtd> <msub> <mi>V</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mn>47.6</mn><mo>*</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>*</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>*</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow> <mrow> <mn>70</mn><mo>*</mo><msup> <mrow> <mn>10</mn></mrow> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> <mo>*</mo><mn>13.6</mn><mo>*</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>*</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow> </mfrac> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mrow></mrow> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>s</mi><mtext> </mtext><msub> <mi>P</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo><msub> <mi>P</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mn>70</mn><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mi>c</mi><mi>m</mi><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mi>o</mi><mi>f</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>H</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>70</mn><mo>*</mo><msup> <mn>10</mn> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> <mo>*</mo><mn>13.6</mn><mo>*</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>*</mo><mn>10</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mrow></mrow> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> </math>
<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0</mn> <msup> <mtext>cm</mtext> <mn>3</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <msup> <mtext>cm</mtext> <mn>3</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q46. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A diesel engine takes in 1 mole of air at 300 K, 1 atm pressure and compresses it adiabatically to <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>th </mtext></mrow></math>of the original volume. Considering air as a diatomic ideal gas, the change in temperature is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>900</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1200</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2400</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>900</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Using       <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <msubsup> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msubsup> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msubsup> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mtext>T</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext>T</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mtext>V</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mtext>V</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mtext>T</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext>T</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>32</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>900</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q47. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Find the minimum attainable pressure of an ideal gas in process <math> <mrow> <mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo><mi>α</mi><msup> <mi>V</mi> <mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></math>, where <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math> and <math> <mi>α</mi> </math> are positive constants and <math> <mi>V</mi> </math> is the volume of one mole of gas.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi><msqrt> <mrow> <mi>α</mi><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow>  <mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi><msqrt>   <mrow>    <mi>&#x03B1;</mi><msub>     <mi>T</mi>     <mn>0</mn>    </msub>    </mrow>  </msqrt>  </mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow>  <mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi><msqrt> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>α</mi><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi><msqrt> <mrow> <mi>α</mi><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given,

<math> <mrow> <mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo><mi>α</mi><msup> <mi>V</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>...(i)

For 1 mol of a gas, <math> <mrow> <mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow> </math>

of <math> <mrow> <mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow> <mi>P</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

Substituting this value in Eq. (i), we get

<math> <mrow> <mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo><mi>α</mi><msup> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow> <mi>P</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo><mi>α</mi><mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mi>R</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <msup> <mi>T</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mi>P</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

or <math> <mrow> <mi>T</mi><msup> <mi>P</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msup> <mi>P</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo><mi>α</mi><msup> <mi>R</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <msup> <mi>T</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

or <math> <mrow> <mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt> <mi>α</mi> </msqrt> <mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><msup> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <mi>T</mi><mo>−</mo><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> ...(ii)

After differentiating, we get

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>d</mi><mi>P</mi></mrow> <mrow> <mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><msqrt> <mi>α</mi> </msqrt> <mi>R</mi><mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <mi>T</mi><mo>−</mo><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> <mo>−</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <mi>T</mi><msup> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <mi>T</mi><mo>−</mo><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </math>

For minimum pressure,

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>d</mi><mi>P</mi></mrow> <mrow> <mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>∴    </mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mi>α</mi> </msqrt> <mo>R</mo> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>/</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>T</mtext> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mo>/</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

After solving, T = 2T<sub>0</sub>

From Eq. (ii),

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>P</mi> <mrow> <mi>min</mi></mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo><msqrt> <mi>α</mi> </msqrt> <mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>−</mo><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi><msqrt> <mrow> <mi>α</mi><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>


             <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0030784_Ans.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 200px; height: 154px;" />

</details>

### Q48. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An ideal monatomic gas is confined in a cylinder by a spring-loaded massless piston of cross-section <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>. The piston can slide on the walls of the cylinder without any friction. Initially, the gas is at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> and occupies a volume of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.4</mo><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and the spring is in its relaxed position. Now, gas is slowly heated by a small electric heater and the piston moves out slowly by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.1</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. Calculate the final temperature of the gas and the heat supplied by the heater. The force constant of the spring is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8000</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and atmospheric pressure is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0629599.jpg" style="width: 301px; height: 131px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>680</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>800</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>720</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>800</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>720</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>800</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>720</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In the initial condition</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pa</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p>Finally,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mi>A</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Pa</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math>, on solving we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>800</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p>Applying work-energy theorem for the piston</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>gas</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>atmos</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>spring</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>gas</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>A</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>120</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#916;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></msub><mo>&#916;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#916;</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>s</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn><mn>0&#160;</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q49. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

When gas is given heat <math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi></math>, a part of heat energy is utilized into work done <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi></math> by gas and the remaining part is utilized to change in internal energy. An ideal diatomic gas is heated at constant pressure,  the ratio of the internal energy change to heat energy supplied, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Fraction of supplied energy which increases the internal energy is given by 

         <math><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>μ</mi><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>μ</mi><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> 

        [here, according to definition of sp. Heat at constant pressure  <math><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>T</mi></math> ] 

        For diatomic gas <math><mi>γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> 

 <math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q50. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>An ideal monoatomic gas undergoes an expansion from state <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> to state <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> following a process which is shown in the indicator diagram. If initially during the expansion, the gas was absorbing heat and later on it was rejecting heat, then what was the volume of the gas when it started rejecting the heat?</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image2-a9fbc9ad-9fe1-4c13-acba-a9b6c6a7a9e3.jpg" style="width: 200px; height: 151px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>12</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>From the graph we can find the pressure of the gas in terms of volume</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mi>V</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><msup><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mi>V</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mi>V</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>V</mi></mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q51. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The molar heat capacity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> of an ideal gas in a process is given by <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math> is constant. If the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>p</mi></msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub></mfrac></math> is known for the gas, then the work done by one mole of gas during heating from <math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> to <math><mi>&#951;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> through the same process will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>a</mi></mfrac><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mi>&#x3B7;</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mi>&#x3B7;</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>O</mi></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#x3B7;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#x3B3;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mi>&#x3B7;</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#x3B3;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>η</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mi>&#x3B7;</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>O</mi></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#x3B7;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#x3B3;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8747;</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><munderover><mo>&#8747;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>o</mi></msub><mrow><mi>&#951;</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>o</mi></msub></mrow></munderover><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mi>T</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mi>&#951;</mi></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#951;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>ln</mi><mfenced><mi>&#951;</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>O</mi></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#951;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>

</details>

### Q52. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of an ideal monatomic gas at temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> expands slowly according to the law <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>V</mi></math> (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi></math> is constant). If the final temperature is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> then heat supplied to gas is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>V</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

</details>

### Q53. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A cyclic process is shown in the figure. Work done by the syatem during the cyclic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ABCDA</mi></math> is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/adfad.JPG" style="width: 200px; height: 138px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>160</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 81px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>150</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 55px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 55px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 55px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 55px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>900</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 55px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 55px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>150</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 55px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 55px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done during the cyclic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ABCDA</mi></math>
= area enclosed by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ABCDA</mi></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>AB</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>CD</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>×</mo> <mtext>AD</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>×</mo> <mn>1</mn> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mtext>.</mtext><mn>5</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mtext>J</mtext></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>150</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014185_AnsExp2_1.jpg" style="width: 200px; height: 141px;" />

</details>

### Q54. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Temperature of an ideal gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The final temperature of the gas when its volume changes from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> in the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>αV</mi></math> (here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">α</mi></math> is a positive constant) is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>900</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1200</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1200</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given relation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mo>∝</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mo>∝</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> changes from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>, pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math> is also doubled

For an ideal gas, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>pV</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo></math>constant

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∴</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>∝</mo><mi>pV</mi></math>. Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> becomes <math><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math> times

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>300</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1200</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q55. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An ideal gas expands isothermally from volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and then it is adiabatically compressed back to its original volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>. The initial and final pressures of the gas are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> respectively and the net work done by the gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi></math>, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&gt;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The two processes are shown in the following P-V diagram:


             <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0029609_Ans.jpg" style="width: 211px; height: 189px;" />


For iothermal process:

         P<sub>1</sub>V<sub>1</sub> = P<sub>2</sub>V<sub>2</sub>

i.e.,      <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

For adiabatic process:

            <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>3</mn> </msub> <msubsup> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>1</mn> <mi>γ</mi> </msubsup> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msubsup> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> <mi>γ</mi> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>

i.e.           <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>3</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mi>γ</mi> </msup> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

As <math> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>&gt;</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </math>, hence P<sub>3</sub> &gt; P<sub>1</sub>

Further, as slope of adiabatic curve is greater than that of isothermal process curve, adiabatic curve will lie above the isothermal curve. That is, area under adiabatic curve &gt; area under isothermal curve

i.e., Negative work &gt; Positive work

i.e., W &lt; 0

</details>

---

### Q56. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A gas is undergoing an adiabatic process. At a certain stage, the volume and absolute temperature of the gas are <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> and the magnitude of the slope of the V-T curve is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math>. molar specific heat of the gas at constant pressure is [Assume the volume of the gas is taken on the y-axis and absolute temperature of the gas taken on x-axis]

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

For adiabatic process <math><mi>T</mi><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant Differentiating with respect to T

<math> <msup> <mi>V</mi> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo><mi>T</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><msup> <mi>V</mi> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow> <mrow> <mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn> </math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mi>, </mi><msub><mrow><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo></math><math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi></math>

<math><mi>γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>P</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>R</mi></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>V</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>V</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q57. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A glass bulb of the volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> is connected to another bulb of volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> by means of a tube of negligible volume. The bulbs contain dry air and are both at a common temperature and pressure of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo> </mo><mi>atm</mi></math>, respectively. The larger bulb is immersed in steam at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> and the smaller in melting ice at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. Find the final common pressure.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 100px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>23</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 96px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>43</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 76px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>53</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 86px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>atm</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 100px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let n<sub>1</sub> and n<sub>2</sub> denote the number of moles of gas in the large and small bulbs, in the final configuration, respectively. Denoting the final temperatures by T<sub>1</sub> and T<sub>2</sub> and the final pressure by P<sub>f</sub>, the ideal gas law implies that

          <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>p</mtext> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>n</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>             ...(i)

and    <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>p</mtext> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>n</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>             ...(ii)

where p<sub>0</sub>, V<sub>0</sub> = V<sub>1</sub> + V<sub>2</sub> and T<sub>0</sub> are the initial pressure, volume, and temperature, respectively.

              <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>p</mtext> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>p</mtext> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>p</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

Solving for Pf, we obtain <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>p</mtext> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>p</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

Inserting the numerical values; p<sub>0</sub> = 1.00 atm, T<sub>0</sub> = 293.15 K(t<sub>0</sub> = 20<sup>o</sup>C),

 T<sub>1</sub> = 373.15 K(t<sub>1</sub> = 100<sup>o</sup>C), and T<sub>2</sub> = 237.15 K(t<sub>2</sub> = 0<sup>o</sup>C),

we find   <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>p</mtext> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>1.00</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>600</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>293.15</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>373.15</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>+</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>273.15</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>1.13 atm</mtext></mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q58. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If <math><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> is an isothermal, <math><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math> is an isochoric and <math><mi>A</mi><mi>C</mi></math> is an adiabatic, which of the graphs correctly represents given in figure?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image2-d7f132a0-1ead-4743-a3e3-8c995e47d5a9.jpg" style="width: 211px; height: 221px;" />
- **(B)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image3-f6969aea-840e-4243-abab-a67d2923a4d0.jpg" style="width: 215px; height: 209px;" />
- **(C)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image4-f3db6a24-0abd-4553-ae72-a4fb0e34e54f.jpg" style="width: 211px; height: 210px;" />
- **(D)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image5-5f9ca48f-ec92-4edf-88e9-de3bfd8b77ef.jpg" style="width: 223px; height: 206px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image3-f6969aea-840e-4243-abab-a67d2923a4d0.jpg" style="width: 215px; height: 209px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

As slope of adiabatic <math><mi>A</mi><mi>C</mi></math> is more than the slope of isothermal <math><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math>, and <math> <mrow>  <mtext>BC</mtext></mrow></math> is isochoric (<math><mi>i</mi><mi>e</mi></math> at constant volume), therefore, figure (b) represents the curves correctly

</details>

### Q59. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A diatomic ideal gas is heated at constant volume until the pressure is doubled and again heated at constant pressure until the volume is doubled. The average molar heat capacity for the whole process is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>9</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>7</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>9</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let initial pressure, volume and temperature be P<sub>0</sub>, V<sub>0</sub> and T<sub>0</sub>, respectively, indicated by state A in P-V diagram. The gas is then isochorically taken to state B (2P<sub>0</sub>, V<sub>0</sub>, 2T<sub>0</sub>) and then taken from state B to state C (2P<sub>0</sub>, 2V<sub>0</sub>, 4T<sub>0</sub>) isobarically.


             <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0029739_Ans..jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 237px; height: 195px;" />


Total heat absorbed by 1 mol of gas

          <math> <mrow> <mo>Δ</mo> <mtext>Q</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>v</mtext> </msub> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>P</mtext> </msub> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

                  <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>×</mo> <mn>2</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>9</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

Total change in temperature from series A to C is <math> <mrow> <mo>Δ</mo> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

Therefore,

Molar heat capacity <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mo>Δ</mo> <mtext>Q</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>Δ</mo> <mtext>T</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>9</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>9</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q60. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A monatomic ideal gas sample is given heat <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></math>. One half of this heat is used as work done by the gas and rest is used for increasing its internal energy. The equation of process in terms of volume and temperature is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo></math> constant
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo></math> constant
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>VT</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo></math> constant

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo></math> constant**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>PdV</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>nC</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></msub><mi>dT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfrac><mi>nRT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac><mi>dV</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>nC</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></msub><mi>dT</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mi>dV</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mi>dT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∴</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>CT</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>, Where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is a constant.</p>

</details>

### Q61. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Corresponding to the process shown in figure, the heat given to the gas in the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. Find value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>.

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/anjana.PNG" style="width: 390px; height: 230px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

Cycle is clockwise. Hence heat will be given to the gas, as work done will be positive.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo></math> area of cycle = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q62. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A litre of dry air at STP allowed to expand to a volume of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> under adiabatic conditions. If <math><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.40</mn></math>, the work done is <math><mo>(</mo><msup><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1.4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4.6555</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>48</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 38px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60.7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 38px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>90.5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 38px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100.8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 38px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>90.5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 38px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Here, <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> atm <math><mo>=</mo><mn>1.013</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.40</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mtext>W</mtext><mo>=</mo><mo>?</mo></math>

As changes are adiabatic,

<math><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><msubsup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msubsup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi></mrow></msubsup></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>1.4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4.6555</mn></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>4.6555</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1.013</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4.6555</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>0.217</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

Work done, W<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mtext>W=</mtext><mfrac><mrow><mn>1.013</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>0.217</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>1.4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math> <mrow>  <mtext>W</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>90.5</mn><mo>&#x00A0;</mo><mtext>J</mtext></mrow></math>

</details>

### Q63. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider an ideal gas confined in an isolated closed chamber. As the gas undergoes an adiabatic expansion, the average time of collision between molecules increases as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>q</mi></msup></math> , where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math> is the volume of the gas. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>q</mi></math> is:

<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Average time of collision 

 <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi><mi> </mi><mi>f</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>e</mi><mi> </mi><mi>p</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>λ</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>v</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>e</mi><mi> </mi><mi>s</mi><mi>p</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi><mi> </mi><mo>(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac></math> 

 <math><mi>t</mi><mo>∝</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> 

 <math><mo>∵</mo><mi>λ</mi><mo>∝</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>o</mi><mo>.</mo><mi> </mi><mi>o</mi><mi>f</mi><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>u</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>s</mi><mi> </mi><mi>p</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>r</mi><mi> </mi><mi>u</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>t</mi><mi> </mi><mi>v</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>u</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> 

 <math><mi>λ</mi><mo>∝</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math> 

 <math><mo>⇒</mo><mi>λ</mi><mo>∝</mo><mi>V</mi></math> 

And <math><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mi> </mi><mo>∝</mo><msqrt><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow></msup><mi>v</mi></msqrt></math> 

So average time

 <math><mo>∴</mo><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>v</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>∝</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></math> 

 <math><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>v</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>∝</mo><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi> </mi></mrow></msup></math> 

 <math><msub><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>v</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>∝</mo><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math> 

 <math><mo>∴</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q64. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A non-conducting piston of mass m and are <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> divides a non-conducting, closed cylinder as shown in the figure. A piston having mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is connected with the top wall of the cylinder by a spring of force constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi></math>. The top part is evacuated and the bottom part contains an ideal gas at a pressure <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> in the equilibrium position. Adiabatic exponent <math><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi></math> and in equilibrium length of each part is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi></math>. (neglect friction). Find the angular frequency for small oscillation.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image1-7b80bd6b-c36a-4459-a6a4-30a9de9a51c8.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mi>k</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>n</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mi mathvariant="italic">k</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">m</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mi>k</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mi>k</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

Restoring force <math><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mi>k</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></msqrt><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>
