### Q65. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of a diatomic gas undergoes a process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mo>[</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>]</mo></mrow></mfrac></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> are constants. The translational kinetic energy of the gas when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>35</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mtext>P</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext> <mo>/</mo> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>3</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

Therefore, translational kinetic energy is equal to

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q66. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of an ideal diatomic gas is taken through a process whose <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> diagram is shown in the figure. The work done by the gas is

<img height="117" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ebsiaoyqzguripolstvhnkfkpaoarcdpqlsjqzzbwhvmpdvuxo.jpeg" width="212" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#x3C0;P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#x3C0;P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#x3C0;P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#x3C0;P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#x3C0;P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done = Area under the curve

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

</details>

### Q67. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A sound wave passing through the air at NTP produces a pressure of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>001</mn><mo> </mo><mi>dyne</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> during a compression. The corresponding change in temperature (given <math> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>1.5</mtext> </mrow> </math> and assume gas to be ideal) is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 172px; top: 41px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>87</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>87</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mtext>T</mtext><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><msup><mo>P</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></msup></math>= constant</p><p>Differentiating

         <math> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi> <msup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mtext>dT</mtext> <msup> <mo>P</mo> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&#947;</mi> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mi>&#947;</mi> </msup> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&#947;</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <msup> <mtext>P</mtext> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&#947;</mi> </mrow> </msup> <mtext>dP</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

or,  <math> <mrow> <mtext>dT</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>T</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi> <mtext>P</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>dP</mtext> </mrow> </math>

or,  <math> <mrow> <mtext>dT</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>1.5</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>1.5</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mn>7</mn> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> <mn>6</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mtext>13.6</mtext> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>9</mn> <mn>8</mn> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mtext>0.001</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q68. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<math><mi>P</mi></math> - <math><mi>V</mi></math> diagram of an ideal gas is shown. The gas undergoes from initial state <math> <mi>A</mi> </math> to final state <math> <mi>B</mi> </math> such that initial and final volumes are same. Select the correct alternative for given process <math> <mrow> <mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow> </math>.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/335280q.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 161px; height: 131px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** Work done by gas is positive
- **(B)** Work done by gas is negative
- **(C)** Temperature of gas increases continuously
- **(D)** Process is isochoric

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Work done by gas is negative**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/335280s.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 142px; height: 125px;" />

Volume does not remain constant throughout the process <math> <mrow>  <mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow></math>. As <math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>, temperature decreases initially as both <math> <mi>P</mi></math> and <math> <mi>V</mi></math> decreases. By area under curve, net work done is negative.

</details>

### Q69. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

During an adiabatic process, the pressure of a gas is found to be proportional to the cube of its absolute temperature. The ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></msub></mfrac></math>  for the gas is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

In an adiabatic process, <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext mathvariant="italic">T</mtext> <mi>γ</mi> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>constant</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <msup> <mtext mathvariant="italic">P</mtext> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>
or  <math>  <mrow> <msup> <mtext mathvariant="italic">T</mtext> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>/</mo> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>constant</mtext> </mrow></mfenced><mtext mathvariant="italic"> P</mtext> </mrow> </math>

Given <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext mathvariant="italic">T</mtext> <mn>3</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>constant</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext mathvariant="italic"> P</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>∴</mo> </mrow> </math>  <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mo>⇒</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mi>γ</mi> </mrow> </math>

or  <math>  <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mo>⇒</mo> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mo>/</mo> <mn>2</mn></mrow></math>

For monoatomic gas, <math> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> = 1.67

For diatomic gas, <math> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> = 1.4

when <math> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> </mrow> </math> = 1.5, the gas must be a suitable mixture of monoatomic and diatomic gases

<math> <mrow> <mo>∴</mo> </mrow> </math>  <math>   <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> </mrow> </math> = 3/2.

</details>

---

### Q70. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of a perfect gas, initially at a pressure and temperature of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>, respectively, expands isothermally until its volume is doubled and then adiabatically until its volume is again doubled. Find the final pressure of the gas. Given <math> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>1.4</mtext> </mrow> </math>.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0036010_Question.jpg" style="width: 231px; height: 174px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>
- **(B)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>
- **(C)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>
- **(D)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> be the initial pressure, volume and the temperature of the gas, respectively. For isothermal expansion (1 to 2), we have

         <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

         <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mfenced><mfrac bevelled="true"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac bevelled="true"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<br.>  </br.>
        <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>

For adiabatic expansion (2 to 3),

    <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">P</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><msubsup><mtext mathvariant="italic">V</mtext><mn>2</mn><mi>&#947;</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">P</mtext><mn>3</mn></msub><msubsup><mtext mathvariant="italic">V</mtext><mn>3</mn><mi>&#947;</mi></msubsup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">V</mtext><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">V</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">P</mtext><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">P</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">V</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">V</mtext><mn>3</mn></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mtext>0.5</mtext><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mn>0</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mfenced><mtext>0.5</mtext></mfenced><mtext>1.4</mtext></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
 </p>

</details>

### Q71. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Temperature and volume curves are drawn for two thermodynamic processes. For the first process, it is a straight line and for the second, it is a rectangular hyperbola. The ratio of work done in the first process to the work done in the second process is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0013421_Question_1.jpg" style="width: 350px; height: 141px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2:1
- **(B)** 3:1
- **(C)** 4:1
- **(D)** 3:2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 2:1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0013421_AnsExp_1.jpg" style="width: 140px; height: 120px;" />
The first process is a constant pressure process, hence
w<sub>1</sub> = nR(2T<sub>0</sub> - T<sub>0</sub>) = nRT<sub>0</sub>
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0013421_AnsExp_2.jpg" style="width: 140px; height: 129px;" />
The equation for the second process is
<math><mrow><mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>c</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext> </mrow></mfrac></mrow></math>
Hence, <math> <mrow> <mtext>P</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>nRT</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>nRc</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></math>
<math> <mrow> <mo>∴    </mo> <mtext>w</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <munderover> <mo>∫</mo> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </munderover> <mtext>PdV</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>nRT</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>∴   </mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>w</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>w</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2 </mn> <mtext>: </mtext> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </math>
∴        2 : 1

</details>

### Q72. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A diatomic molecule can be modelled as two rigid balls connected with a spring such that the balls can vibrate with respect to the centre of mass of the system (spring + balls). Consider a diatomic gas made of such diatomic molecules. If the gas performs <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> of work under isobaric condition, then heat given to the gas (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>) is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **70**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>nC</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>nR</mi><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>20</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"> <mrow> <mo>=70</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>J</mtext></mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q73. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An ideal gas <math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.5</mn><mo>)</mo></math> is expanded adiabatically. How many times its initial volume should the gas be expanded to reduce the root mean square velocity of molecules by a factor of <math><mrow><mtext>2</mtext></mrow></math>?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math > <mtext>4</mtext></math> times
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>16</mtext></mrow> </math> times
- **(C)** <math> <mtext>8</mtext></math> times
- **(D)** <math> <mtext>2</mtext></math> times

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math> <mrow> <mtext>16</mtext></mrow> </math> times**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>RT</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mfrac></msqrt><mo>  </mo><mo>  </mo><mo>  </mo><mo>  </mo><mo>  </mo><mo>  </mo><mi>or</mi><mo>  </mo><mo>  </mo><mo>  </mo><mo>  </mo><mo>  </mo><mo>  </mo><mo>  </mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub><mo>∝</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></msqrt></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi>rms</mi></msub></math> is to reduce two times, i.e. the temperature of the gas will have to reduce four times or

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">'</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>

During the adiabatic process,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><msup><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mo>⁡</mo><mo>'</mo></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><msup><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo>'</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><msup><mo>⁡</mo><mo>'</mo></msup></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">'</mi></msup></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></msup></math>

<math><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1.5</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">'</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q74. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Two soap bubbles of radii x and y coalesce to constitute a bubble of radius z. Then z is equal to</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msqrt><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math><msqrt><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><msqrt><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<math><mi>p</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0029179_Ans.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="opacity: 0.9; width: 200px; height: 62px;" />

<math><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

If the process takes place is vacuum then <math><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

If process is isothermal

<math><mo>&there4;</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>p</mi><mi>V</mi></math>

<math><mo>&there4;</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></math>

</details>

### Q75. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An ideal gas expands isothermally from a volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and then compressed to original volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> adiabatically. The initial pressure is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and final pressure is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>. The total work done is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi></math>. Then:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the graph it is clear that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> .
Since the area under adiabatic process <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is greater than that of the isothermal process <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> . Therefore net word done
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>A</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/bf8690b9-59e2-48b0-8772-05ff4a5040a6.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width:2.07774in;height:1.70755in" />

</details>

---

### Q76. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two gases occupy two containers <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>; the gas in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, of volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>, exerts a pressure of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>40</mn><mo> </mo><mi>MPa</mi></math> and that in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>, of volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>, exerts a pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo> </mo><mi>MPa</mi></math>. The two containers are joined by a tube of negligible volume and the gases are allowed to intermingle. Then if the temperature remains constant, the final pressure in the containers will be (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MPa</mi></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>90</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>98</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>40</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>10</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>98</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

As the quantity of gas remains constant <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&#956;</mi> <mtext>A</mtext> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mi>&#956;</mi> <mtext>B</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mi>&#956;</mi> </mrow> </math>

        <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mtext>V</mtext><mtext>A</mtext></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

        <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext>1.4</mtext><mo>&#215;</mo><mtext>0.1</mtext><mo>+</mo><mtext>0.7</mtext><mo>&#215;</mo><mtext>0.15</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext>0.1</mtext><mo>+</mo><mtext>0.15</mtext></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mtext>0.98 MPa</mtext></math>

</details>

---

### Q77. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An ideal gas is taken through the cycle  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>A</mtext><mo>→</mo><mtext>B</mtext><mo>→</mo><mtext>C</mtext><mo>→</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, as shown in the figure. If the net heat supplied to the gas in the cycle is <math> <mrow> <mn>5</mn><mtext> </mtext><mtext>J</mtext></mrow></math>, the work done by the gas in the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>C</mtext><mo>→</mo><mtext>A</mtext></math>  is
<span style="line-height: 1.6em;">   </span><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0006066_Ques1_1.jpg" style="line-height: 1.6em; width: 287px; height: 228px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>5</mn><mtext> J</mtext></mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow>  <mo>&#x2212;</mo><mn>10</mn><mtext>&#x00A0;J</mtext></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow>  <mo>&#x2212;</mo><mn>15</mn><mtext>&#x00A0;J</mtext></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow>  <mo>&#x2212;</mo><mn>20</mn><mtext>&#x00A0;J</mtext></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>5</mn><mtext> J</mtext></mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>AB</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mn>p</mn> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mn>V</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>10 J</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#916;</mo><msub><mtext>W</mtext><mtext>BC</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mtext>as V = constant</mtext></mfenced></math>

From first law of thermodynamics,

<math>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>Q</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mn>W</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mn>U</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>U</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0 &#160; &#160; &#160; &#160;</mn> </mrow> </math> 

Process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ABCA</mi></math> is cyclic,

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo>&#160; <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>Q</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mn>W</mn> <mn>AB</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mn>W</mn> <mn>BC</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mn>W</mn> <mn>CA</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo>&#160; <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mn>W</mn> <mn>CA</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>Q</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mn>W</mn> <mn>AB</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mn>W</mn> <mn>BC</mn> </mrow> </math>

                  <math><mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>5 J</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q78. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of a monoatomic ideal gas undergoes the process <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>⟶</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>, as in the given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></math> diagram. The specific heat for this process is


<img alt="" height="156" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image1-67d0f115-9220-4087-92cb-02cd662289cb.jpg" width="226" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>15</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>30</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>15</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></math> (From <math><msup><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msup></math> law)

Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math>

Temperature at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math>

At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>16</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math>

So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>14</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>21</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>   [Area under <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></math> diagram]

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>21</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>30</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>14</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi></math>

</details>

### Q79. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of an ideal gas is taken along the process in which <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>x</mi></msup></math> = constant. The graph shown represents the variation of molar heat capacity of such a gas with respect to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>. The values of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>'</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>'</mo></math>. respectively, are given by

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/Question_No._66.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 250px; height: 150px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> <mtext>, </mtext> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> <mtext>, </mtext> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> <mtext>, </mtext> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> <mtext>, </mtext> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> <mtext>, </mtext> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

For <math> <mrow>  <mi>P</mi><msup>   <mi>V</mi>   <mi>x</mi>  </msup>  <mo>=</mo><mtext>constant</mtext></mrow></math> 

<math> <mrow>  <mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><msub>   <mi>C</mi>   <mi>V</mi>  </msub>  <mo>+</mo><mfrac>   <mi>R</mi>   <mrow>    <mo>&#x2212;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow>  </mfrac>  </mrow></math>

At <math> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>∞</mo> <mtext>,  </mtext> <mtext>C</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

from <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>PV</mtext> <mtext>x</mtext> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>constnat</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒   </mo> <msup> <mtext>P</mtext> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>/</mo> <mtext>x</mtext> </mrow> </msup> <mtext>V</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>another constant</mtext> </mrow> </math>

so at <math> <mrow> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>∞</mo> <mtext>,  </mtext> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>v</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

hence <math> <mrow> <mtext>C</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>v</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

and then <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>p</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>v</mtext> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

at x = 0, P = constant and C = C'

hence <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>C</mtext> <mo>′</mo> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>p</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

at x = x', C = 0, so the process is adiabatic, hence

    <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>x</mtext> <mo>′</mo> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>p</mtext> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>v</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q80. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Diagram shows the variation in the internal energy U with the volume V of 2.0 moles of an ideal gas in cyclic process abcda. The temperatures of the gas at b and c are 500 K and 300 K, respectively. Calculate the heat absorbed by the gas during the process.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0030796_Question.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 200px; height: 181px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** 400 R ln 2
- **(B)** 500 R ln 2
- **(C)** 700 R ln 2
- **(D)** 800 R ln 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 400 R ln 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

In the process a to b and c to d

as <math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>U</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>, therefore <math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math> or T = constant

                  <math> <mrow> <mtext>W</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <munderover> <mo>&#8747;</mo> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mtext>i</mtext> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> </msub> </munderover> <mtext>PdV</mtext> </mrow> </math>

We have,

                <math> <mrow> <mtext>PV</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>nRT</mtext> <mo>&#8658;</mo> <mtext>P</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>nRT</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo> <mtext>W</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <munderover> <mo>&#8747;</mo> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mtext>i</mtext> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> </msub> </munderover> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>nRT</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>RV</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

                  <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>nRT</mtext> <msubsup> <mrow> <mfenced close="|" open="|" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>ln V</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mtext>i</mtext> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> </msub> </msubsup> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>nRT</mtext> <mo>ln</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mtext>i</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

          <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>ab</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>nRT</mtext> <mtext>b</mtext> </msub> <mo>ln</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>ln</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>R ln 2</mo> </mrow> </math>

and   <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>cd</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>nRT</mtext> <mtext>c</mtext> </msub> <mo>ln</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>ln</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>6</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>R ln 2</mo> </mrow> </math>

There is no volume change from b to c and from d to a, so

              <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>bc</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>da</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

The work done in the complete cycle

       <math> <mrow> <mtext>W</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>ab</mtext> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>bc</mtext> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>cd</mtext> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>da</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </math>

           <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn><mo>R ln 2</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn><mo>R ln 2</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

           <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>R ln 2</mo> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q81. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A sound wave passing through the air at NTP produces a pressure of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>001</mn><mo> </mo><mi>dyne</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> during a compression. The corresponding change in temperature (given <math> <mrow> <mi>γ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>1.5</mtext> </mrow> </math> and assume gas to be ideal) is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 172px; top: 41px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>87</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>87</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mtext>T</mtext><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><msup><mo>P</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></msup></math>= constant</p><p>Differentiating

         <math> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi> <msup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mtext>dT</mtext> <msup> <mo>P</mo> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&#947;</mi> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mi>&#947;</mi> </msup> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&#947;</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <msup> <mtext>P</mtext> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&#947;</mi> </mrow> </msup> <mtext>dP</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

or,  <math> <mrow> <mtext>dT</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>T</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi> <mtext>P</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>dP</mtext> </mrow> </math>

or,  <math> <mrow> <mtext>dT</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>1.5</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>1.5</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mn>7</mn> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> <mn>6</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mtext>13.6</mtext> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>9</mn> <mn>8</mn> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mtext>0.001</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>97</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q82. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Air is filled in a bottle at atmospheric pressure and it is corked at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>35</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mn>C</mn></math>. If the cork can come out at a pressure <math><mn>3 </mn></math>times the atmospheric pressure, then up to what temperature should the bottle be heated in order to remove the cork.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>325.5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mn>C</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>851</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mn>C</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>651</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mn>C</mn></math>
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>651</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mn>C</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

We know that, <math><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>35</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>273</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>308</mn></math> ....(i)

and <math><msub><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> ....(ii)

On dividing Equation (ii) by Equation (i), we get

<math><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>308</mn></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>924</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>K</mn></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>924</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>273</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>651</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mn>C</mn></math>

</details>

---

### Q83. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Three samples of the same gas <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> (<mi></mi><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>) have equal volume initially. Now, the volume of each sample is doubled. For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, the process is adiabatic; for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>, it is isobaric and for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>, the process is isothermal. If the final pressures are equal for all the three samples, the ratio of their initial pressures is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext> : </mtext> <mn>1</mn> <mtext> : </mtext> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mtext> : </mtext> <mn>1</mn> <mtext> : </mtext> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mtext> : </mtext> <mn>1</mn> <mtext> : </mtext> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mtext> : </mtext> <mn>2</mn> <mtext> : </mtext> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mtext> : </mtext> <mn>1</mn> <mtext> : </mtext> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let P<sub>A</sub>, P<sub>B</sub>, P<sub>C</sub> be the initial pressures of the three samples and P be the final pressure of each.

For A process is adiabatic, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>&#947;</mi></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo></math>constant

<mo></mo><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>(</mo><mi>V</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>V</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>P</mi></math>

For B, the process is isobaric  <mo></mo><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>P</mi></math>

For C, the process is isothermal, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo></math> constant

<mo></mo><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>V</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>P</mi></math>

Hence, <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mtext>A</mtext> </msub> <mtext>&#160;:&#160;</mtext> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mtext>B</mtext> </msub> <mtext>&#160;:&#160;</mtext> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mtext>C</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msup> <mn>2</mn> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>/</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mtext>&#160;:&#160;</mtext> <mn>1</mn> <mtext>&#160;:&#160;</mtext> <mn>2</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mtext>&#160;:&#160;</mtext> <mn>1</mn> <mtext>&#160;:&#160;</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q84. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Diagram shows the variation of internal energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>)</mo></math> with the pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>)</mo></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mole</mi></math> gas in cyclic process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>abcda</mi></math>. The temperature of gas at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>, respectively. Calculate the heat absorbed by the gas during the process.


                <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0030772_Question.jpg" style="width: 225px; height: 208px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>440</mn><mo> </mo><mi>R</mi><mo> </mo><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo> </mo><mi>R</mi><mo> </mo><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>430</mn><mo> </mo><mi>R</mi><mo> </mo><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>414</mn><mo> </mo><mi>R</mi><mo> </mo><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo> </mo><mi>R</mi><mo> </mo><mi>ln</mi><mn>2</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Change in internal energy for cyclic process <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>U</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

For process <math> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>&#160;&#8594;&#160;</mo> <mtext>b</mtext> <mtext>,&#160;&#160;</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>P</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>constant</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

     <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>&#160;&#8594;&#160;</mo> <mtext>b</mtext> </mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>P</mtext> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>V</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>n</mtext> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>&#160;R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

For process <math> <mrow> <mtext>b</mtext> <mo>&#160;&#8594;&#160;</mo> <mtext>c</mtext> <mtext>,&#160;&#160;</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>constant</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

     <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext>W</mtext><mrow><mtext>b</mtext><mo>&#160;&#8594;&#160;</mo><mtext>c</mtext></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>nRTln</mi><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext>R</mtext><mfenced><mn>300</mn></mfenced><mtext>ln</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

For process <math> <mrow> <mtext>c</mtext> <mo>&#160;&#8594;&#160;</mo> <mtext>d</mtext> <mtext>,&#160;&#160;</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>P</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>constant</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

     <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext>W</mtext><mrow><mtext>c</mtext><mo>&#160;&#8594;&#160;</mo><mtext>d</mtext></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mtext>P</mtext><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>V</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext>nR</mtext><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>T=</mtext><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn><mtext>R</mtext></math>

For process <math> <mrow> <mtext>d</mtext> <mo>&#160;&#8594;&#160;</mo> <mtext>a</mtext> <mtext>,&#160;&#160;</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>constant</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

     <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext>W</mtext><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mo>&#160;&#8594;&#160;</mo><mtext>a</mtext></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>nRTln</mi><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext>R</mtext><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mtext>ln</mtext><mn>&#160;2</mn></math>

Net work,

    <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>W</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mrow> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>&#160;&#8594;&#160;</mo> <mtext>b</mtext> </mrow> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mrow> <mtext>b</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>c</mtext> </mrow> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mrow> <mtext>c</mtext> <mo>&#160;&#8594;&#160;</mo> <mtext>d</mtext> </mrow> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mrow> <mtext>d</mtext> <mo>&#160;&#8594;&#160;</mo> <mtext>a</mtext> </mrow> </msub> </mrow> </math>

      <math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>W</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>&#160;R ln 2</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>dQ</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>dU</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>dW</mtext> </mrow> </math>, first law of thermodynamics

 <math> <mrow> <mtext>dQ</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>&#160;R ln 2</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>
